```
## §P-TAPE-SUBTRACTION — FROZEN (s328, Michael GO) — the reframe's first forward contact

> The stacked-exposure frame's first pre-registerable edge. The reframe
> retrodicts everything and predicts nothing yet; this is the contact
> that lets it win, lose, or be reframed. Standing guard active (s324):
> no capture treats the frame as true before a pre-registered win.

### Claim under test (queue binary → sharpened)

Does contrary evidence on the tape SUBTRACT from the standing own-class
license (signed integrator — matches the weight-face GC2 cancellation)
or PILE UP alongside (competing stacks — the own-class level, once
established, survives contradiction)?

**⚠ Sharpening (Michael GO s328; tests are the AI's job).** Read
literally, the binary is nearly *pre-decided by trivial ICL*:
anti-class statements about `w` mechanically lower anti-predicate
surprisal, so "contrary subtracts" passes for free and teaches nothing.
The informative, distinctive test is **order-sensitivity**, which
trivial ICL does not predict and which adjudicates the §Synthesis spine
on the tape:

- A **signed integrator is commutative** — 3 own + 3 anti nets to ~0
  *regardless of order*.
- **Early-commitment** (§Synthesis: sign decided early is permanent) is
  **order-sensitive** — the first-committed class survives (primacy).
- **Trivial recency ICL** is the mirror — the *last* statements win
  (anti-first > own-first).

Three mechanisms → three pre-registered order-signatures. The order
arms are **content-matched** (identical 3 own-membership + 3
anti-membership statements; only sequence differs) so the make-or-break
carries no lexical confound.

### Register (λ measure)

LICENSING — value register, graded. `L = mean surprisal(anti preds) −
mean surprisal(own preds)`, signed by `w`'s nominal class `c`
(idempotency `_signed_L`, the tape face that landed s315). Correct
register for a standing-magnitude claim; a crisp/routing probe would
manufacture crispness (λ measure). The nominal class `c` is just the
readout axis — the physics is symmetric across the mix (net lean toward
`c`), which is exactly what the order arms probe.

### Substrate + reuse (λ one_way, no fork)

idempotency (`L_at`, `coherent_prefix`, `incoherent_stmts`,
`_member_stmts` via `tw`, `_signed_L`, `REAL_MEMBERS`, `HELD_PREDS`,
`REAL_MARGIN_FLOOR`) · type_icl_tag (`signed_T`, `class_axes`,
`band_layers`, `BAND_DEPTH` — the landed §11 T instrument, for the
second-substrate corroboration) · holo_cap (`NONCE_CANDS`) ·
verbum.dsp.nulls (`gate`, `NullDraws`, `paired_permutation`,
`sign_flip`). Anti-membership statements = `_member_stmts(w, 1−c)` —
structurally identical to own-membership.

### Construction (nonce w, nominal class c; k_own = 3 fixed, k_anti swept)

k_own = 3 is the idempotency sweet spot (solid standing license,
pre-dip; the k=4,5 atypical-template caveat avoided). Arms:

- `OWN-ONLY` — [own×3], k_anti=0 (the standing level; TS0 + reference).
- `OWN+FILLER` — [own×3] + [incoherent×k_anti], k_anti∈{1,2,3} — the
  token-matched DILUTION baseline (membership-free filler; no class
  edge).
- `MIX-OWNFIRST` — [own×3][anti×k_anti], k_anti∈{1,2,3} — the
  subtraction curve.
- At the balanced point (3 own + 3 anti), two extra orderings:
  `MIX-ANTIFIRST` — [anti×3][own×3]; `MIX-INTERLEAVED` — alternating
  (neutral primacy).

Read `L` (signed, nominal class `c`) per arm per k per nonce.

### Gates

- **TS0 SANE (void-gate):** `L(own-only,3) > 0` solidly; real-member
  anchor `real_margin ≥ REAL_MARGIN_FLOOR` ∧ per-class ok; register
  works (L rises k=0→3 own). Void if not.
- **TS2 SUBTRACTION-DEPTH (gates NO-EROSION):** `L(interleaved,3+3) <
  L(filler,3+3)` paired per nonce → balanced contradiction erodes below
  neutral dilution (genuine signed work). If not → own license immune.
- **TS1 ORDER (make-or-break, 3-way):** stat = `L(own-first) −
  L(anti-first)` at balance, paired-permutation null. Sign selects the
  mechanism.
- **TS3 DC-COROBORATION (advisory, 2nd substrate):** the same TS1/TS2
  signs read on the type_icl_tag **T** register (class-axis projection
  at a constant probe frame `" The {w}."`, band L(0.50–0.85)). Reports;
  never gates. Two-substrate confirm (as idempotency did weight-plate +
  tape).

### Verdict tree (frozen)

```
¬TS0                        → VOID
¬TS2 (no erosion)           → NO-EROSION        # own license immune → strongest pile-up
TS2 ∧ TS1 not-sig (blind)   → SIGNED-INTEGRATOR # subtracts, commutative → own does NOT survive balance
TS2 ∧ TS1 > 0 (sig)         → EARLY-COMMITMENT   # own-first survives → §Synthesis forward-win on the tape
TS2 ∧ TS1 < 0 (sig)         → RECENCY-BUFFER     # anti-first wins → last-wins; frame-damaging
```

### Verdicts + a-priori (NOT tuned; modal on the mundane)

- **SIGNED-INTEGRATOR 30** — subtraction observed, order-blind. Matches
  the weight-face GC2 signed cancellation; the literal *add-only
  photographic-stack* reading is falsified in its strongest form, the
  deeper §Synthesis camera reading upheld on the tape.
- **RECENCY-BUFFER 30** — anti-first wins. Neither integrator nor
  commitment; a recency window. FRAME-DAMAGING (a stack averages all
  frames, not just recent; reframes the s326 DC accumulate-and-hold as
  possibly a recency artifact). The frame can lose here — standing guard
  satisfied.
- **EARLY-COMMITMENT 20** — own-first survives, anti-first does not.
  Competing-stacks with primacy; a pre-registered FORWARD WIN for the
  sign-is-the-decision spine on the tape (early commitment is durable).
  Kept modest — the frame must earn it.
- **NO-EROSION 10** — own license immune to contradiction regardless of
  order. Maximal pile-up.
- **VOID 10** — register fails to form.

### Read discipline (banked at freeze)

- The make-or-break is the ORDER sign (TS1), not the trivial subtraction
  (which TS2 confirms is present at all). Do NOT read a bare subtraction
  as a frame win.
- SIGNED-INTEGRATOR and EARLY-COMMITMENT both cohere with the licensed
  §Synthesis spine (camera / sign-committed); RECENCY-BUFFER damages the
  stacked-exposure frame; NO-EROSION is the strongest pile-up. Report the
  measured signs; let the tree speak.
- Bounds: single model (Qwen3-4B), L-register (surprisal licensing) with
  T-register corroboration, single-context (block-free) timescale,
  k_own=3 fixed (one exposure depth). EARLY-COMMITMENT would license
  "first-committed class survives balanced contradiction on this
  substrate" — not unbounded durability.
```

```
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""§P-TAPE-SUBTRACTION — does contrary tape evidence subtract or pile up?

Pre-reg: mementum/knowledge/explore/types-are-a-modulation-scheme.md
§P-TAPE-SUBTRACTION (FROZEN s328, Michael GO).

The stacked-exposure reframe's first forward contact. A signed integrator
is COMMUTATIVE (3 own + 3 anti net to ~0 regardless of order); early
commitment is ORDER-SENSITIVE (first-committed class survives, primacy);
trivial recency ICL is the mirror (last statements win). The order arms
are content-matched (identical 3 own + 3 anti membership statements, only
sequence differs) so the make-or-break carries no lexical confound.

Register (lambda measure) = LICENSING (value, graded): L = mean
surprisal(anti preds) - mean surprisal(own preds), signed by w's nominal
class c (idempotency _signed_L, the tape face that landed s315). T-register
corroboration (type_icl_tag signed_T) rides along as an advisory second
substrate (TS3).

Construction (nonce w, nominal class c; k_own = 3 fixed, k_anti swept):
  OWN-ONLY      [own x3]                     standing level (TS0 + ref)
  OWN+FILLER    [own x3][filler xk] k in 1,2,3  token-matched dilution
  MIX-OWNFIRST  [own x3][anti xk]   k in 1,2,3  subtraction curve
  MIX-ANTIFIRST [anti x3][own x3]            balanced, anti primacy
  MIX-INTERLEAVED [own,anti,...]             balanced, neutral primacy

Gates: TS0 SANE (void) . TS2 SUBTRACTION-DEPTH (gates NO-EROSION:
L(interleaved,3+3) < L(filler,3+3)) . TS1 ORDER (make-or-break 3-way:
sign of L(own-first) - L(anti-first) at balance, two signed one-sided
gates) . TS3 DC-COROBORATION (advisory, T register).

Verdict tree: ¬TS0 → VOID · ¬TS2 → NO-EROSION · TS2 ∧ TS1>0 →
EARLY-COMMITMENT · TS2 ∧ TS1<0 → RECENCY-BUFFER · TS2 ∧ TS1 order-blind →
SIGNED-INTEGRATOR. A-priori (NOT tuned): SIGNED-INTEGRATOR 30 /
RECENCY-BUFFER 30 / EARLY-COMMITMENT 20 / NO-EROSION 10 / VOID 10.

Reuse (λ one_way, no fork): type_write (_member_stmts, HELD_PREDS, CLASSES,
REAL_MEMBERS, _signed_L, REAL_MARGIN_FLOOR) · idempotency (incoherent_stmts)
· type_icl_tag (signed_T, class_axes, band_layers) · holo_cap (NONCE_CANDS)
· verbum.jlens · verbum.dsp.nulls (gate, NullDraws, paired_permutation).

License: MIT (lambda provenance).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import sys
import time
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

_HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
if str(_HERE) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(_HERE))
_WRAP = _HERE.parents[1] / "wrapper"
if str(_WRAP) not in sys.path:
    sys.path.insert(0, str(_WRAP))

import idempotency as idem  # noqa: E402  (frozen s320 — incoherent filler)
import type_icl_tag as ti  # noqa: E402  (frozen §10 — T instrument)
import type_write as tw  # noqa: E402  (frozen §8 — constants + pure fns)
from holo_cap import NONCE_CANDS  # noqa: E402

from verbum.dsp.nulls import (  # noqa: E402
    Register,
    gate,
    paired_permutation,
)

# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# Frozen constants
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
K_OWN = 3                       # fixed own-class exposures (idempotency sweet spot)
K_ANTI = (0, 1, 2, 3)           # swept contrary/filler exposures
N_NULL = 10_000
ALPHA = 0.05
APRIORI = {"SIGNED-INTEGRATOR": 30, "RECENCY-BUFFER": 30,
           "EARLY-COMMITMENT": 20, "NO-EROSION": 10, "VOID": 10}


# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# Prefix construction (pure) — content-matched order arms
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
def own_stmts(w: str, c: int, k: int) -> list[str]:
    return tw._member_stmts(w, c)[:k]


def anti_stmts(w: str, c: int, k: int) -> list[str]:
    return tw._member_stmts(w, 1 - c)[:k]


def filler_stmts(w: str, k: int) -> list[str]:
    return idem.incoherent_stmts(w)[:k]


def _join(stmts: list[str]) -> str:
    return (" ".join(stmts) + " ") if stmts else ""


def own_only_prefix(w: str, c: int) -> str:
    return _join(own_stmts(w, c, K_OWN))


def own_filler_prefix(w: str, c: int, k_anti: int) -> str:
    return _join(own_stmts(w, c, K_OWN) + filler_stmts(w, k_anti))


def mix_ownfirst_prefix(w: str, c: int, k_anti: int) -> str:
    return _join(own_stmts(w, c, K_OWN) + anti_stmts(w, c, k_anti))


def mix_antifirst_prefix(w: str, c: int) -> str:
    return _join(anti_stmts(w, c, K_OWN) + own_stmts(w, c, K_OWN))


def mix_interleaved_prefix(w: str, c: int) -> str:
    o, a = own_stmts(w, c, K_OWN), anti_stmts(w, c, K_OWN)
    seq: list[str] = []
    for j in range(K_OWN):
        seq.append(o[j])
        seq.append(a[j])
    return _join(seq)


# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# Gates (pure — --validate plants the bundle; no torch, no model)
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
def _sane(b: dict) -> dict:
    """TS0 void-gate: standing own-class license + real anchor + register."""
    L_own_only = np.asarray(b["L_ownfirst"], float)[:, 0]   # k_anti=0 = own-only
    standing = float(L_own_only.mean())
    m = b.get("metric", {})
    real_ok = (m.get("real_margin", 0.0) >= tw.REAL_MARGIN_FLOOR
               and bool(m.get("per_class_ok", False)))
    register_ok = bool(standing > 0.0)
    return {"gate": "TS0", "standing_L": standing, "real_ok": real_ok,
            "register_ok": register_ok,
            "pass": bool(real_ok and register_ok)}


def _ts2(b: dict, rng: np.random.Generator, n_null: int = N_NULL) -> dict:
    """SUBTRACTION-DEPTH: L(filler,3+3) - L(interleaved,3+3) > 0 (erosion)."""
    filler_bal = np.asarray(b["L_filler"], float)[:, -1]    # k_anti=3
    inter = np.asarray(b["L_interleaved"], float)
    obs = float(np.mean(filler_bal - inter))
    null = paired_permutation(filler_bal, inter, rng, n_iter=n_null)
    g = gate(obs, null, "greater", ALPHA, "TS2_subtraction_depth",
             claim_register=Register.value, probe_register=Register.value)
    return {"gate": "TS2", "pass": bool(g.verdict), "erosion": obs, "p": g.p,
            "L_filler_bal": float(filler_bal.mean()),
            "L_interleaved": float(inter.mean())}


def _ts1(b: dict, rng: np.random.Generator, n_null: int = N_NULL) -> dict:
    """ORDER make-or-break (3-way): sign of L(own-first) - L(anti-first).

    Two signed one-sided gates against the same paired null (mutually
    exclusive tails): primacy (>0 -> EARLY-COMMITMENT) vs recency
    (<0 -> RECENCY-BUFFER). Neither -> order-blind -> SIGNED-INTEGRATOR."""
    ownfirst_bal = np.asarray(b["L_ownfirst"], float)[:, -1]   # [own x3][anti x3]
    antifirst = np.asarray(b["L_antifirst"], float)            # [anti x3][own x3]
    obs = float(np.mean(ownfirst_bal - antifirst))
    null = paired_permutation(ownfirst_bal, antifirst, rng, n_iter=n_null)
    g_prim = gate(obs, null, "greater", ALPHA, "TS1_primacy",
                  claim_register=Register.value, probe_register=Register.value)
    g_rec = gate(obs, null, "less", ALPHA, "TS1_recency",
                 claim_register=Register.value, probe_register=Register.value)
    return {"gate": "TS1", "order_diff": obs,
            "L_ownfirst_bal": float(ownfirst_bal.mean()),
            "L_antifirst": float(antifirst.mean()),
            "primacy_p": g_prim.p, "primacy_pass": bool(g_prim.verdict),
            "recency_p": g_rec.p, "recency_pass": bool(g_rec.verdict)}


def _ts3(b: dict) -> dict:
    """DC-COROBORATION (advisory, T register). Signs only — never gates."""
    if "T_ownfirst" not in b:
        return {"gate": "TS3", "advisory": True, "available": False}
    T_ownfirst = np.asarray(b["T_ownfirst"], float)[:, -1]
    T_antifirst = np.asarray(b["T_antifirst"], float)
    T_filler = np.asarray(b["T_filler"], float)[:, -1]
    T_inter = np.asarray(b["T_interleaved"], float)
    return {"gate": "TS3", "advisory": True, "available": True,
            "T_order_diff": float(np.mean(T_ownfirst - T_antifirst)),
            "T_erosion": float(np.mean(T_filler - T_inter)),
            "T_standing": float(np.asarray(b["T_ownfirst"], float)[:, 0].mean())}


def verdict(ts0: dict, ts2: dict, ts1: dict) -> str:
    if not ts0["pass"]:
        return "VOID"
    if not ts2["pass"]:
        return "NO-EROSION"
    if ts1["primacy_pass"]:
        return "EARLY-COMMITMENT"
    if ts1["recency_pass"]:
        return "RECENCY-BUFFER"
    return "SIGNED-INTEGRATOR"


def compute_gates(b: dict, rng: np.random.Generator,
                  n_null: int = N_NULL) -> dict:
    ts0 = _sane(b)
    ts2 = _ts2(b, rng, n_null)
    ts1 = _ts1(b, rng, n_null)
    ts3 = _ts3(b)
    v = verdict(ts0, ts2, ts1)
    # curve means for reporting
    def cm(key):
        return [float(np.asarray(b[key], float)[:, j].mean())
                for j in range(np.asarray(b[key], float).shape[1])]
    return {
        "verdict": v, "a_priori": APRIORI,
        "gates": {"TS0": ts0, "TS1": ts1, "TS2": ts2, "TS3": ts3},
        "means": {
            "curve_ownfirst": cm("L_ownfirst"),   # [own x3][anti xk]
            "curve_filler": cm("L_filler"),        # [own x3][filler xk]
            "L_antifirst": float(np.asarray(b["L_antifirst"], float).mean()),
            "L_interleaved": float(np.asarray(b["L_interleaved"], float).mean()),
            "n_nonce": int(np.asarray(b["L_ownfirst"], float).shape[0]),
        },
    }


# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# --validate: planted worlds (no model)
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
def _world(rng, kind: str, n: int = 24) -> dict:
    K = len(K_ANTI)
    metric = {"real_margin": 2.5, "per_class_ok": True}
    nz1 = lambda s=0.08: rng.normal(0.0, s, n)           # noqa: E731

    def ramp(start, end):
        """(n,K) linear from start(k=0) to end(k=3) + noise."""
        line = np.linspace(start, end, K)
        return np.tile(line, (n, 1)) + rng.normal(0.0, 0.06, (n, K))

    # baselines shared by all live worlds
    L_filler = ramp(2.0, 1.7)          # mild neutral dilution, stays high
    L_ownfirst = ramp(2.0, 2.0)        # placeholder; balanced endpoint set below
    L_antifirst = 2.0 + nz1()
    L_interleaved = 2.0 + nz1()

    if kind == "signed_integrator":
        L_ownfirst = ramp(2.0, 0.0)    # subtracts to ~0
        L_antifirst = 0.0 + nz1()      # order-blind: same as own-first endpoint
        L_interleaved = 0.0 + nz1()
    elif kind == "early_commitment":
        L_ownfirst = ramp(2.0, 1.7)    # own-first SURVIVES contradiction
        L_antifirst = 0.1 + nz1()      # anti-first erodes own license
        L_interleaved = 0.8 + nz1()    # balanced, below filler
    elif kind == "recency":
        L_ownfirst = ramp(2.0, 0.1)    # own-first erodes (anti is last → last wins)
        L_antifirst = 1.7 + nz1()      # anti-first HIGH (own is last → own wins)
        L_interleaved = 0.8 + nz1()
    elif kind == "no_erosion":
        L_ownfirst = ramp(2.0, 1.75)   # immune
        L_antifirst = 1.75 + nz1()
        L_interleaved = 1.72 + nz1()   # ≈ filler → TS2 fails
    elif kind == "void":
        L_ownfirst = ramp(2.0, 0.0)
        metric = {"real_margin": -0.3, "per_class_ok": False}
    else:
        raise ValueError(kind)

    return {"L_filler": L_filler, "L_ownfirst": L_ownfirst,
            "L_antifirst": L_antifirst, "L_interleaved": L_interleaved,
            "metric": metric}


def run_validate() -> int:
    print("── §P-TAPE-SUBTRACTION --validate (planted worlds, no model) ──")
    want = {"signed_integrator": "SIGNED-INTEGRATOR",
            "early_commitment": "EARLY-COMMITMENT",
            "recency": "RECENCY-BUFFER",
            "no_erosion": "NO-EROSION",
            "void": "VOID"}
    ok = True
    for kind, expect_v in want.items():
        rng = np.random.default_rng(hash(kind) % (2**31))
        res = compute_gates(_world(rng, kind), rng, n_null=2000)
        good = res["verdict"] == expect_v
        ok &= good
        g = res["gates"]
        print(f"  {kind:18s} -> {res['verdict']:18s} expect {expect_v:18s} "
              f"[TS2 p={g['TS2']['p']:.3f} TS1 prim_p={g['TS1']['primacy_p']:.3f} "
              f"rec_p={g['TS1']['recency_p']:.3f}] {'✓' if good else '✗ FAIL'}")

    # ── primitives ──
    w, c = "wug", 0
    # (1) order arms are content-matched multisets (only sequence differs)
    of = own_stmts(w, c, K_OWN) + anti_stmts(w, c, K_OWN)
    af = anti_stmts(w, c, K_OWN) + own_stmts(w, c, K_OWN)
    prim1 = sorted(of) == sorted(af) and of != af
    ok &= prim1
    print(f"  primitive order content-match     {'✓' if prim1 else '✗ FAIL'}")

    # (2) anti statements assert the OTHER class (differ from own)
    prim2 = set(own_stmts(w, c, K_OWN)).isdisjoint(set(anti_stmts(w, c, K_OWN)))
    ok &= prim2
    print(f"  primitive anti≠own                {'✓' if prim2 else '✗ FAIL'}")

    # (3) token-budget parity at balance: ownfirst / antifirst / interleaved
    #     all carry 6 statements; filler-balanced also 6 (3 own + 3 filler)
    def n_stmt(pfx: str) -> int:
        return pfx.count(".")
    bals = [mix_ownfirst_prefix(w, c, K_OWN), mix_antifirst_prefix(w, c),
            mix_interleaved_prefix(w, c), own_filler_prefix(w, c, K_OWN)]
    prim3 = len({n_stmt(p) for p in bals}) == 1 and n_stmt(bals[0]) == 2 * K_OWN
    ok &= prim3
    print(f"  primitive token-budget parity     {'✓' if prim3 else '✗ FAIL'}")

    # (4) own-only == ownfirst(k=0) == filler(k=0) construction identity
    prim4 = (own_only_prefix(w, c) == mix_ownfirst_prefix(w, c, 0)
             == own_filler_prefix(w, c, 0))
    ok &= prim4
    print(f"  primitive own-only k=0 identity   {'✓' if prim4 else '✗ FAIL'}")

    # (5) interleaved alternates own,anti,own,anti,...
    o0 = own_stmts(w, c, K_OWN)[0]
    a0 = anti_stmts(w, c, K_OWN)[0]
    inter = mix_interleaved_prefix(w, c)
    prim5 = inter.index(o0) < inter.index(a0) < inter.index(own_stmts(w, c, K_OWN)[1])
    ok &= prim5
    print(f"  primitive interleave alternation  {'✓' if prim5 else '✗ FAIL'}")

    print(f"\n── --validate {'ALL PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'} ──")
    return 0 if ok else 1


# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
# Model path
# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
def run_model(args) -> int:
    import torch
    import torch.nn.functional as F
    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer

    from verbum import jlens

    dev = (args.device if (args.device != "mps"
                           or torch.backends.mps.is_available()) else "cpu")
    rng = np.random.default_rng(args.seed)
    tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(args.model_id)
    if tok.pad_token is None:
        tok.pad_token = tok.eos_token
    tok.padding_side = "right"
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
        args.model_id, dtype=getattr(torch, args.dtype)).to(dev).eval()
    for p in model.parameters():
        p.requires_grad_(False)
    nl = jlens.n_layers(model)
    tband = ti.band_layers(nl)
    out_dir = Path(args.out)
    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    print(f"[ts] {args.model_id} dev={dev} n_layers={nl} "
          f"T-band=L{tband[0]}..L{tband[-1]} K_OWN={K_OWN} "
          f"K_ANTI={list(K_ANTI)}", flush=True)

    # ── L instrument (surprisal licensing) ──
    def surprisal(prefix: str, cont: str) -> float:
        pre = tok(prefix, return_tensors="pt").to(dev)
        full = tok(prefix + cont, return_tensors="pt").to(dev)
        n_pre = pre.input_ids.shape[1]
        with torch.no_grad():
            lo = model(**full).logits[0].float()
        lp = F.log_softmax(lo, dim=-1)
        tgt = full.input_ids[0]
        return -sum(float(lp[pos - 1, tgt[pos]])
                    for pos in range(n_pre, tgt.shape[0]))

    def L_at(prefix: str, w: str, c: int) -> float:
        pre = prefix + f"The {w}"
        sA = np.mean([surprisal(pre, " " + p) for p in tw.HELD_PREDS[0]])
        sV = np.mean([surprisal(pre, " " + p) for p in tw.HELD_PREDS[1]])
        return float(tw._signed_L(np.array([sA]), np.array([sV]),
                                  np.array([c]))[0])

    # ── T instrument (class-axis projection at constant probe frame) ──
    def capture_band_at(text: str, positions: list[int]) -> np.ndarray:
        resid, _ids = jlens.capture_residuals(model, tok, text)
        return np.stack([np.stack([resid[li][p].numpy() for li in tband])
                         for p in positions])

    def T_at(prefix: str, w: str, c: int, axes: np.ndarray) -> float:
        text = prefix + f"The {w}"
        pos = len(tok(text).input_ids) - 1        # last token (= w)
        h = capture_band_at(text, [pos])          # (1, L, d)
        return float(ti.signed_T(h, axes, np.array([c]))[0])

    # ── nonce selection (type_write / type_icl_tag pattern) ──
    nonces, labels = [], []
    for i, w in enumerate(NONCE_CANDS):
        n_the = tok("The", add_special_tokens=False).input_ids
        n_thew = tok(f"The {w}", add_special_tokens=False).input_ids
        if len(n_thew) - len(n_the) >= 1:
            nonces.append(w)
            labels.append(i % 2)
    if args.n_nonce:
        a = [j for j, x in enumerate(labels) if x == 0][:args.n_nonce // 2]
        v = [j for j, x in enumerate(labels) if x == 1][:args.n_nonce // 2]
        sel = sorted(a + v)
        nonces = [nonces[j] for j in sel]
        labels = [labels[j] for j in sel]
    labels = np.array(labels, int)
    print(f"[ts] nonces={len(nonces)} (animal {int((labels == 0).sum())} "
          f"vehicle {int((labels == 1).sum())})", flush=True)

    # ── real-member class axes (T) + licensing anchor (L, TS0 SANE) ──
    real_members = list(tw.REAL_MEMBERS[0]) + list(tw.REAL_MEMBERS[1])
    real_labels = np.array([0] * len(tw.REAL_MEMBERS[0])
                           + [1] * len(tw.REAL_MEMBERS[1]))
    print("[ts] real-member anchor + axes …", flush=True)
    rA, rV, h_members = [], [], []
    for w in real_members:
        pre = f"The {w}"
        rA.append(np.mean([surprisal(pre, " " + p) for p in tw.HELD_PREDS[0]]))
        rV.append(np.mean([surprisal(pre, " " + p) for p in tw.HELD_PREDS[1]]))
        h_members.append(capture_band_at(pre, [len(tok(pre).input_ids) - 1])[0])
    L_real = tw._signed_L(np.array(rA), np.array(rV), real_labels)
    metric = {
        "real_margin": float(np.mean(L_real)),
        "per_class_ok": bool(np.mean(L_real[real_labels == 0]) > 0
                             and np.mean(L_real[real_labels == 1]) > 0),
    }
    axes = ti.class_axes(np.stack(h_members), real_labels)
    print(f"[ts] real margin={metric['real_margin']:.3f} "
          f"per_class_ok={metric['per_class_ok']}", flush=True)

    # ── sweep: L (+ T) per arm per nonce ──
    K = len(K_ANTI)
    L_filler = np.zeros((len(nonces), K))
    L_ownfirst = np.zeros((len(nonces), K))
    L_antifirst = np.zeros(len(nonces))
    L_interleaved = np.zeros(len(nonces))
    T_filler = np.zeros((len(nonces), K))
    T_ownfirst = np.zeros((len(nonces), K))
    T_antifirst = np.zeros(len(nonces))
    T_interleaved = np.zeros(len(nonces))
    t0 = time.time()
    for ni, (w, lb) in enumerate(zip(nonces, labels, strict=True)):
        c = int(lb)
        for kj, kk in enumerate(K_ANTI):
            L_filler[ni, kj] = L_at(own_filler_prefix(w, c, kk), w, c)
            L_ownfirst[ni, kj] = L_at(mix_ownfirst_prefix(w, c, kk), w, c)
            T_filler[ni, kj] = T_at(own_filler_prefix(w, c, kk), w, c, axes)
            T_ownfirst[ni, kj] = T_at(mix_ownfirst_prefix(w, c, kk), w, c, axes)
        L_antifirst[ni] = L_at(mix_antifirst_prefix(w, c), w, c)
        L_interleaved[ni] = L_at(mix_interleaved_prefix(w, c), w, c)
        T_antifirst[ni] = T_at(mix_antifirst_prefix(w, c), w, c, axes)
        T_interleaved[ni] = T_at(mix_interleaved_prefix(w, c), w, c, axes)
        if (ni + 1) % 4 == 0:
            print(f"[ts] swept {ni + 1}/{len(nonces)} "
                  f"({time.time() - t0:.0f}s)", flush=True)

    b = {"L_filler": L_filler, "L_ownfirst": L_ownfirst,
         "L_antifirst": L_antifirst, "L_interleaved": L_interleaved,
         "T_filler": T_filler, "T_ownfirst": T_ownfirst,
         "T_antifirst": T_antifirst, "T_interleaved": T_interleaved,
         "metric": metric}
    res = compute_gates(b, rng, n_null=args.n_null)
    res["meta"] = {
        "model_id": args.model_id, "n_nonce": len(nonces),
        "nonces": nonces, "labels": labels.tolist(),
        "k_own": K_OWN, "k_anti": list(K_ANTI), "band": [tband[0], tband[-1]],
        "metric": metric, "n_null": args.n_null,
        "timestamp": time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ", time.gmtime()),
        "elapsed_s": round(time.time() - t0, 1),
    }
    (out_dir / "results.json").write_text(json.dumps(res, indent=2))
    np.savez_compressed(
        out_dir / "curves.npz",
        L_filler=L_filler, L_ownfirst=L_ownfirst, L_antifirst=L_antifirst,
        L_interleaved=L_interleaved, T_filler=T_filler, T_ownfirst=T_ownfirst,
        T_antifirst=T_antifirst, T_interleaved=T_interleaved,
        labels=labels, k_anti=np.array(K_ANTI))

    g, mn = res["gates"], res["means"]
    print(f"[ts] wrote {out_dir}/results.json")
    print(f"[ts] TS0 {g['TS0']['pass']} (standing_L={g['TS0']['standing_L']:.3f}"
          f" real_ok={g['TS0']['real_ok']}) | "
          f"TS2 erosion={g['TS2']['erosion']:.3f} p={g['TS2']['p']:.4f} "
          f"{g['TS2']['pass']}")
    print(f"[ts] TS1 order_diff={g['TS1']['order_diff']:.3f} "
          f"(ownfirst={g['TS1']['L_ownfirst_bal']:.3f} "
          f"antifirst={g['TS1']['L_antifirst']:.3f}) "
          f"primacy_p={g['TS1']['primacy_p']:.4f} "
          f"recency_p={g['TS1']['recency_p']:.4f}")
    print(f"[ts] curve_ownfirst={[round(x, 3) for x in mn['curve_ownfirst']]}")
    print(f"[ts] curve_filler  ={[round(x, 3) for x in mn['curve_filler']]}")
    print(f"[ts] L_antifirst={mn['L_antifirst']:.3f} "
          f"L_interleaved={mn['L_interleaved']:.3f}")
    if g["TS3"].get("available"):
        print(f"[ts] TS3(T) order_diff={g['TS3']['T_order_diff']:.3f} "
              f"erosion={g['TS3']['T_erosion']:.3f} "
              f"standing={g['TS3']['T_standing']:.3f}")
    print(f"[ts] VERDICT: {res['verdict']}")
    print(f"VERDICT: {res['verdict']}")
    return 0


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
    ap.add_argument("--validate", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--model-id", default="Qwen/Qwen3-4B")
    ap.add_argument("--device", default="mps")
    ap.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16",
                    choices=["float32", "bfloat16"])
    ap.add_argument("--n-nonce", type=int, default=0)
    ap.add_argument("--n-null", type=int, default=N_NULL)
    ap.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=328)
    ap.add_argument("--out", default="results/tape-subtraction/qwen3-4b")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    if args.validate:
        return run_validate()
    return run_model(args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    raise SystemExit(main())
```

### Thinking

I'm designing §P-ORDER-RECONCILE to investigate whether the divergence between behavioral licensing (L) and representational class-axis (T) reflects genuine register dissociation—where behavioral decisions commit early while representation tracks recent evidence—or if it's an artifact of how the bands are measured. I need to read both L and T at point M to determine which interpretation holds. L measures surprisal differences across held predicates, while I'm considering two competing explanations: either there's a genuine dissociation where early decisions favor primacy but graded representation tracks recency, or the difference stems from methodological factors like depth, position, or how the axes are constructed. To test this, I could read T across all layers including the final ones to see if the recency signal flips to primacy in later layers—which would suggest the dissociation is really a depth phenomenon—or match the position by reading T at the continuation point instead of the token itself.

I'm also noticing a strange non-monotone pattern in the own-first curve when sweeping trailing contradictions: it starts at 2.96, drops to −0.05, stays low at −0.13, then bounces back to 0.35. This suggests that within-arm recency effects (where trailing contradictions erode the signal) coexist with an overall primacy bias, so I could design an experiment that varies where a single contradictory statement sits in the sequence to map out this recency profile more precisely.

The cleaner way to test whether this is genuine dissociation or just an artifact is to use the two-register framework: if it's real, then even when I match the measurement positions and depths, the primacy signal should still show up in one register while recency shows in the other. But if it's an artifact, matching the instruments should collapse the split. I can test this by capturing residuals across all layers and computing the signed recency signal per layer to see if the pattern holds at different depths. So I'd compute a per-layer log-likelihood at the predicate positions by applying the model's final normalization and unembedding to residuals at each depth, giving me L as a function of layer depth. This lets me check whether L reads recency at the band-depth (suggesting the decision only crystallizes in late layers, with mid-layer representations already tracking recency) and whether T reads primacy in late layers (the same story from the opposite direction). The tricky part is that L and T aren't measured at the same positions or with the same projections—L uses unembedding at predicate positions in held frames, while T uses class-axis projection at the w position—so I need to be careful about what "matched positions" means when comparing across depths.

The s206 experiment showed that logit-lens caught something attention weights missed, so registers really do matter. My minimal design is to fix the prefix conditions (the two order arms at balance, plus reference conditions) and then sweep a grid of measurement instruments to see which one best adjudicates whether this is an artifact or genuine: I'll compute T per layer across all depths, then...

Actually, I need to align the positions carefully. T reads at the w token, but logit-lens reads at the predicate positions. So I should measure T at the predicate-frame position too—specifically where w sits within the prefix that logit-lens uses, so they're reading the same residual stream at the same point.

The real comparison is a 2×2 grid: I can either read out via the class axis or via unembed surprisal, and I can sweep across layers or just use the final layer. That gives me T(ℓ) for per-layer axis readout and LL(ℓ) for per-layer logit-lens surprisal—though the latter is more expensive since I need to compute log-softmax over the vocabulary at each layer.

I'm predicting two possible outcomes: either the order effect is fundamentally a depth phenomenon where both readouts show recency in mid-layers and primacy in late layers, suggesting the decision crystallizes at some commitment depth; or the two readouts genuinely diverge, revealing a true register split between them. I'm designing an experiment where I vary the position of a single contradictory item within a sequence of identical content—placing it first, second, third, or last—to measure how recency distance affects the loss signal. The prediction is that earlier contradictions (further from readout) should cause less erosion than trailing ones, which would support the within-arm recency plus net primacy hypothesis.

But the s328 data complicates this: when the anti item came first, the loss was nearly erased rather than strongly negative, which contradicts pure primacy logic. This suggests the actual mechanism is more nuanced than either recency or primacy alone—the class commitment isn't simply determined by the first statement.

I should run a clean sweep varying position across a 3:1 own-to-anti ratio to isolate what matters: whether position is commutative within the arm, whether trailing items hurt most through a recency kernel, or whether leading anti truly blocks commitment below what trailing would do. The computational cost is manageable—a few minutes with per-layer logit lens captures to track how the model's predictions evolve across all layers at the held-out positions.

The critical test is whether this order effect is genuine register dissociation or just an artifact of the measurement instrument. I'm setting up a 2×2 factorial design crossing order differences with readout method (transformer logits vs. language model logits at different layers), then pre-registering the key gates: does the s328 split replicate under both instruments, with primacy showing positive order differences in the transformer readout and recency showing negative differences in the language model readout?

For replication fidelity, I'm using the same deterministic nonce selection (20 nonces via label-based indexing) so the model should produce near-identical outputs across runs. The real make-or-break test is whether the sign of the order effect flips depending on readout method and layer depth — if the language model readout at band depth shows recency dominance while the transformer readout at later layers shows primacy dominance, that's strong evidence for genuine register dissociation rather than instrument artifact. The key distinction is whether the depth sweep can separate a genuine two-register dissociation—where decision and evidence operate at different depths—from an instrument artifact where the axis construction or readout method itself creates the appearance of a split. The depth-cross diagnostic pinpoints where any divergence lives, and comparing whether depth or readout explains the pattern tells us if we're seeing a real cognitive dissociation or just an artifact of how the model's representations are being measured. The readout basis itself might carry the sign rather than depth, or both factors could interact in a genuinely entangled way. There's also the possibility that what looks like primacy is actually an artifact of how the data gets aggregated at specific grain levels—which would still fall under the depth-commitment category. I'm settling on four core verdicts: VOID, DEPTH-COMMITMENT, REGISTER-DISSOCIATION, and ENTANGLED, with the within-arm recency profile serving as additional context rather than a gating criterion.

Now I'm testing whether recency effects within a single arm can explain the non-monotone curve mechanically. I'm sweeping through positions where the contradicting item appears and measuring how the L register changes as that item moves further back in the sequence. If L is higher when the contradiction appears early and drops as it moves later, that's a recency kernel at work—showing that both recency and primacy can coexist on the same register. This within-arm profile acts as supporting evidence for that reading.

I'm now considering what the underlying mechanism actually is. The T erosion pattern in one model and the logit-lens history suggest depth-commitment could be the clean explanation—decisions crystallizing late in the network. But I need to be careful not to just pick the story I want. Register dissociation is also plausible here, like we've seen before with routing versus value registers. The "boring" outcome would be whatever trivial physics predicts at mid-depth layers, which logit-lens typically shows as weak or noisy signal anyway.

For the modal breakdown, I'm settling on depth-commitment around 30%, register-dissociation at 25%, entangled-or-blind at 25%, void at 10%, and leaving 10% unaccounted for—though I might fold the underpowered cases into entangled rather than keeping them separate, since the distinction is whether both signals are significant versus neither being significant at the interaction.

Now I'm structuring the verdict gates more carefully: the first gate checks whether the core replication holds (final layer shows positive depth signal, band shows negative temporal signal, both significant with paired permutation test, and the real anchor is present), which either passes as SANE or fails as VOID or NON-REPLICATION depending on which component breaks. The second gate examines the crossed cells to determine if the interaction pattern itself is robust or fragile. Looking at the depth and recency profiles across layers to identify commitment transitions, then checking the recency kernel with a slope statistic. I'm also reconsidering the prior distribution—whether depth-commitment at 30% is justified as the modal case, since late layers typically capture behavioral patterns while band layers reflect more recent activity.

The logit-lens evidence at band depth aligns with this framing, so depth-commitment appears both theoretically sound and consistent with the two-register structure. I need to be careful about what would actually damage this frame—register-dissociation just constrains the space, while split-fragile could undermine the qualified caveat unless the T-recency effect replicates deterministically.

For testing, using the same 20 nonces makes sense for paired comparison and exact anchoring rather than fresh nonces for generalization. The OR0 replication becomes a code-fidelity check rather than a statistical event, since re-running the logit calculation should give identical results up to floating-point noise. That settles it—split-fragile probability collapses to near-zero and folds into void.

Now I'm settling on the final verdict distribution: ENTANGLED-PARTIAL at 35 as the modal outcome since crossing designs often produce messy, partial results, DEPTH-COMMITMENT at 30, REGISTER-DISSOCIATION at 25, and VOID at 10—totaling 100. The mid-band logit lens might be noisy, which could explain why ENTANGLED-PARTIAL dominates.

There's a design question about position alignment: T reads at the w token while L reads at prediction tokens, creating a position mismatch. Rather than expanding the design space to 2×2×2 by adding T at prediction positions (which would require building axes there), I'll treat this position mismatch as a bounded residual effect and note it as such, keeping the design lean. The simplest fix would be reading T at the last context token before the prediction frame, which avoids unnecessary complexity. LL(final) should equal L exactly as a sanity check, and I need to extend T's depth computation with per-layer signed variants using per-layer class axes. For implementation, I'm sketching out the different arm configurations needed—mixing own and anti terms in various balances, plus position sweeps and standing/erosion checks—reusing the tape subtraction patterns.

For each arm, I'll run a forward pass capturing hidden states at every layer, compute per-layer residuals and LL values using the final norm and unembed at prediction positions, then extract per-layer T values. The null hypothesis uses paired permutations across nonces with multiple cells, each with its own gate at α=0.05, focusing on two pre-registered primary gates.

Estimating costs across arms per nonce... The vocab matmul is the bottleneck here — computing full logits across 151k vocabulary at just the prediction target positions. I can optimize by only computing log-softmax at those final few positions per continuation rather than all positions, which brings it down to roughly 115 GFLOP per forward pass. That scales to maybe 30-60 minutes across all nonces, which is acceptable though pushing toward the upper bound. I could subsample layers to speed things up further if needed.

For the late-layer definition, I'm deciding between using just the final layer or aggregating the last 15% of layers. The T band spans layers 18-31, so for a matched-depth comparison I should use the final residual output (layer 36) as my reference point for cell B, comparing it against where the model's actual decision emerges.

Now I'm setting up the primary gates: the SANE condition checks that the real anchor is valid, standing loss is positive, the final layer LL matches the band LL within tolerance, and the signs replicate what we saw in s328. Then OR1a examines the band LL sign and significance using paired permutation testing, with OR1b following a similar pattern.

The verdict tree routes through these gates—if the anchor or replication checks fail, we mark it void or report it as non-replicated. When both the depth and readout signals are significant, we classify the split as either depth-committed or register-dissociated depending on their sign relationship. Otherwise we flag it as entangled-partial and report the full 2×2 map. Finally, I'm tracking the depth profiles across layers to identify where the commitment actually occurs.

Now I'm looking at the recency kernel—comparing the earliest and latest anti-positions to measure how contradiction decays over time, which also accounts for the non-monotone behavior in the k curve. This recency statistic should be pre-registered as a secondary gate since the prediction is clean: trailing positions hurt most, so we expect a positive signal. There's a subtlety here I need to think through more carefully.

The anti-position timing matters differently depending on whether primacy or recency dominates. When anti comes first in a 3:1 own-dominant arm, it might destroy commitment disproportionately under primacy, but under recency, the trailing anti at slot 4 is what causes the most damage. The difference between early and late anti positions is actually a mini order test that discriminates between primacy and recency kernels. Study 328 shows trailing anti is devastating—it nearly erases the effect—which predicts recency should be positive on L. But the same study also showed own-first dominates at the block level, which points to primacy on L. Both signals are on L, so that's where I should focus.

The reconciliation might not just be across L and T, but actually embedded within L itself—recency at the within-arm level and primacy at the block level coexisting. I'm setting up a verdict space with ENTANGLED-PARTIAL and DEPTH-COMMITMENT as the leading candidates, then I need to draft the freeze section on the knowledge page, update the queue status, checkpoint the state, and get Michael's approval before the freeze.

I should present the design proposal to Michael concisely—covering the primary crossing cells definition, whether to include the OR3 secondary gate, confirming the 20 nonces with paired replication, and the a-priori masses—then move through the standard flow: design proposal → Michael's GO → freeze commit → harness validation → smoke test → full run → verdict.…

I've loaded the design substrate with the s328 result, frozen harness, and queue context. Now I'll set up the work plan and bring you the freeze proposal.

## Assistant

## §P-ORDER-RECONCILE — design proposal (pre-freeze, needs your GO)

**The question (from s328's TS3 split):** on the *same prefixes*, L (behavioral licensing, held-pred surprisal) read PRIMACY (+0.478) while T (class-axis projection, band L18–31) read RECENCY (−1.30). Genuine two-register dissociation — *sign(decision)=primacy, magnitude(evidence)=recency* — or instrument artifact?

**The design key (found in the harness, sharpens the queue's "matched positions"):** the position mismatch is nearly nil already. L's first-pred-token surprisal is computed from the logits at position `w` — the *same token position* T reads. The two instruments differ in exactly **two factors**:

| factor | L (s328) | T (s328) |
|---|---|---|
| readout | final-norm + unembed → held-pred surprisal | real-member class-axis projection |
| depth | final layer | band L(0.50–0.85) |

So the reconciliation is a clean **2×2 crossing: {readout} × {depth}**, filling the two missing cells:

- **Cell A — LL(band):** logit-lens L — per-layer residual → final norm + unembed → held-pred surprisal, aggregated over the *same band* T uses. "What does the behavioral readout say at T's depth?"
- **Cell B — T(final):** class-axis projection at the *final* layer (per-layer axes from real members). "What does the axis say at L's depth?"

Identity anchor: LL(final) ≡ L exactly (same computation) — a built-in instrument-fidelity check.

### Gates

- **OR0 SANE (void):** real-member anchor ≥ floor ∧ standing L > 0 ∧ LL(final)≡L (numerical tol) ∧ s328 endpoints replicate on the same 20 nonces (D_L(final) > 0 sig, D_T(band) < 0 sig — deterministic reuse, so failure = instrument drift).
- **OR1 CROSSING (make-or-break):** D = order_diff(own-first-bal − anti-first), paired-perm null per cell:
  - **A < 0 ∧ B > 0** (sign follows DEPTH) → **DEPTH-COMMITMENT** — evidence is recency-tracking at mid-depth, the decision commits late; instruments agree at matched depth. Reconciles the split into a depth law.
  - **A > 0 ∧ B < 0** (sign follows READOUT) → **REGISTER-DISSOCIATION** — genuine: the axis tracks recency at all depths, the behavioral readout is primacy at all depths.
  - anything else → **ENTANGLED-PARTIAL** (report the full 2×2 map + depth profiles).
- **OR2 depth profiles (advisory):** D_T(ℓ) and D_LL(ℓ) for all ℓ → if a sign transition exists, the **commitment depth ℓ\*** — the layer where the decision forms.
- **OR3 RECENCY KERNEL (secondary, pre-registered):** the queue's second component. Single-anti position sweep at 3:1 own-dominance — `[anti,o,o,o] / [o,anti,o,o] / [o,o,anti,o] / [o,o,o,anti]` (content-identical, only slot differs). Stat = L(anti@slot1) − L(anti@slot4), paired perm. **> 0 = trailing contradiction hurts most** = within-arm recency kernel ON L itself — mechanically explains the non-monotone curve [2.96, −0.05, −0.13, 0.35] and quantifies how recency and primacy coexist inside one register. Read on T(band) too (advisory).

### Verdict tree + a-priori (NOT tuned; modal split on mundane)

```
¬OR0(anchor/identity)   → VOID                    10
¬OR0(split replication) → SPLIT-NOT-REPLICATED    10
A<0 ∧ B>0 (both sig)    → DEPTH-COMMITMENT        30
A>0 ∧ B<0 (both sig)    → REGISTER-DISSOCIATION   20
else                    → ENTANGLED-PARTIAL       30
```

Honesty notes, banked at freeze: DEPTH-COMMITMENT is both mundane-lore-modal (decisions form late) *and* frame-friendly (sharpens the two-register law into a depth law) — named, not hidden. Mid-band logit-lens is historically noisy → ENTANGLED co-modal. Neither DEPTH-COMMITMENT nor REGISTER-DISSOCIATION damages the s328 win — both *locate* the split; only SPLIT-NOT-REPLICATED would reopen it.

### Substrate + cost

Same 20 nonces (deterministic selection → paired replication), Qwen3-4B, arms: own-first-bal + anti-first (the crossing) + own-only/filler-bal/interleaved (standing/erosion refs) + 4 single-anti arms (OR3). Full-depth residual capture per forward; per-layer unembed only at pred target positions. Reuses `tape_subtraction` constructions + `type_icl_tag` T instrument, no fork (λ one_way). Est. ~15–45 min on mps — cheap tier.

**Bounds (named now):** single model, single context, k_own=3, residual position mismatch only in multi-token pred tails (dominant read matched at `w`).

```
# Types Are a Modulation Scheme

> s324 hammock (Michael: "superbake showed us how to use DSP tools to work
> with models. What if types are in the signal? If we think of this as a
> holography problem, what techniques could we bring to bear?"). This page
> reads the closed s282–s323 type arc through the s308 optics toolbox
> (holographic-untangling-methods) and the s288 convergence ledger
> (geometry-holography-signals-convergence). The reframe retrodicts two
> closed fingerprints, converts the arc's stubborn negatives into theorems,
> and yields four unfrozen probe candidates plus one novel falsifiable
> prediction. NOTHING here is frozen; s222 law applies.

## The reframe
```

```
# ── logit-lens readouts ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────


@torch.no_grad()
def logit_lens(model: nn.Module, resid: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
    """Map residual STATE(s) ``(..., d)`` to logits ``(..., vocab)`` (full norm)."""
    norm, unembed = _final_norm_unembed(model)
    dtype = next(model.parameters()).dtype
    dev = next(model.parameters()).device
    return unembed(norm(resid.to(dtype).to(dev)))


@torch.no_grad()

) -> list[str]:
```

### Thinking

I see that `jlens.logit_lens` is available for mapping residual states to logits with full final normalization. For the harness, I'm setting up a per-nonce, per-class, per-arm-prefix structure where the text stem combines the prefix with "The {w}", and I need to capture the surprisal of each held-out continuation. My approach is to forward the complete text once with residual capture across all layers, then use logit_lens on each layer's residuals to compute the per-layer log-likelihoods for each continuation.

For the target positions, I'm computing surprisal from the residuals at positions predicting the continuation tokens. The final layer's LL matches exactly what the model produces since logit_lens applies the same norm and unembed operations. For the T (target) component, I can extract it from any single continuation forward at the position of w, since causal attention ensures the residual at that position is identical regardless of whether continuation tokens follow.

My plan is to run 8 forwards per nonce-arm pair (one for each held prediction), capturing the full residual stream. From the first forward, I'll also extract T at position w across all layers. Then I'll compute per-layer signed attention and value contributions to get the signed logit difference per layer, which gives me LL per layer.

For the class axes, I'm taking the mean residuals across the 8 bare "The {m}" members at all layers and the last position, then computing axes per layer as the normalized difference between attention and value means. I'll build an axes array of shape (num_layers, d) and use it with the residual activations to compute per-layer T values via projection and sign. Computing T across all layers and then extracting the band mean and final layer value should be straightforward with a small helper function using the same dot-product and sign logic.

For the real-member anchor, I'm using the same approach as before: bare "The {m}" plus the surprisal at the final layer from the model logits. For the different arms, I need own_only for the standing L baseline, filler_bal for the erosion reference, ownfirst_bal for the crossing case, and then the ant

-interleaved variant plus four single-anti slots where each position gets the antonym once. That's 9 arms total, and with 8 pred-forwards per nonce, I'm looking at 72 forwards per nonce—each capturing all 36 layers plus per-layer logit-lens at a few target positions. The computational cost per forward is one model pass for the capture, then for logit-lens across 36 layers at maybe 1-3 positions, with Qwen3-4B's 151k vocab and 2560 dimensions, that's roughly 0.77 GFLOP per position-layer.

So across ~100 position-layer combinations, I'm adding about 77 GFLOP per forward. On MPS that's a fraction of a second, and the model pass itself is fast on short text, so per nonce I'm looking at roughly 72 forwards × 0.5-1s each, which gives me 40-70 seconds per nonce. For 20 nonces that's 15-25 minutes total, which aligns with my earlier estimate. Memory-wise, storing the residuals per layer on CPU as float32 is minimal.

For optimization, batching the 8 pred continuations would help but padding gets messy, so I'll keep it sequential. Now I need to structure the statistics output—the bundle should contain per-arm per-layer log-likelihoods in an array.

I'm organizing separate arrays for each arm variant: own-first, anti-first, own-only, filler, inter, and slot-based. Even though OR3 only pre-registered the final layer loss, I'm capturing all layers anyway since it's cheap. I'm also computing per-class accuracy metrics and an identity check that compares the final layer loss against a direct logits path to validate consistency.

For the identity gate, I need to verify that the captured residuals path matches what the model would compute directly. Since the final layer loss is just the unembedding of the normalized residual, it should be identical by construction—but to make this a real fidelity check, I'll compute logits via a separate forward pass on the own-only arm and compare. This adds one extra forward per prediction, which doubles the cost for that arm only but is acceptable.

For the gate conditions themselves, I'm setting up the checks: the standing loss should be positive, and the identity check should pass within a tolerance. Given bfloat16 precision, I'll set the tolerance conservatively—mean absolute difference under 0.05 nats and max difference under 0.25—and compute both paths in float to avoid accumulating rounding errors.

Now I'm looking at the replication results: the final layer difference between own-first and anti-first shows a significant paired permutation effect, and the time-band difference also shows significance. I'm organizing the gate conditions into two scenarios—one based on the band-level loss differences with positive and negative gates, another based on the final time difference. Defining the verdict logic... Now I'm mapping out the different failure modes and edge cases: entangled scenarios where signals partially overlap, split cases where replication breaks down, void states when anchors fail, and a kernel verification world. Then I need to generate test arrays for each world, constructing LL and T profiles with the appropriate difference patterns and noise characteristics, including slot-based profiling.

I'm validating that the slot sequences form proper permutations of the same multiset with distinct orderings, checking that the slot4 construction matches the identity formula, and reusing the balanced arm builders from the tape_subtraction module to ensure consistency across ownfirst and antifirst variants.

Now I'm working through the band layer indexing — the band spans layers 18 through 31 (computed as 85% of 36 total layers), which is disjoint from the final layer at index 35, so the residual capture logic should work correctly with the layer indices from band_layers. I need to verify the sign convention for per-layer axes aligns with how the residuals are indexed.

For the slot construction, I'm defining a function that takes an ownership statement and an anti statement, then inserts the anti statement at a specified position (0 through 3) among the ownership statements. When the anti is inserted at position 3, it matches the behavior of mix_ownfirst with k=1, confirming the slot mechanism works as intended.

Now I'm handling nonce selection using the same deterministic loop from tape_subtraction with a default of 20 nonces. For each nonce, I'm computing results across multiple arm configurations—own_only, filler_bal, ownfirst_bal, antifirst, inter, and the four slot variants—storing per-layer L and T values for each. The per-layer computation takes a prefix, appends the target word, tokenizes it to get the prefix length, then accumulates statistics across layers.

For each held prediction, I'm capturing residuals from the model at positions after the prefix, computing logits through logit lens at each layer and timestep, then calculating negative log-softmax to get the cross-entropy cost. The computation scales across 36 layers, 1-3 token positions, and 8 predictions per class, which batches efficiently.

I'm also planning to verify the identity check by computing direct logits separately for the own_only arm using a small helper function, since the residual capture discards the original logits. For replication validation, I'll compare against the s328 baseline numbers to ensure the signs match within this run.

The output will be a results.json file plus curves.npz containing all the arrays, with a printed verdict. I'm setting up the CLI with flags for validation mode, model ID, device, dtype, number of nonces, nulls, seed, and output path. I'll keep the code style consistent with tape_subtraction.py and separate the pure gates for the validation flag.

Writing the output schema... Defining gate functions for different conditions... Now I'm setting up validation worlds with specific parameters—36 layers, a band range from 18 to 32, and 24 samples. I'm constructing synthetic datasets by building baseline log-likelihood and timing profiles, then adding noise and applying the difference profiles to create "own" versus "anti" versions, with filler and inter conditions included for completeness, and slot profiles applied across all layers.

I'm defining three distinct world configurations: depth_commitment applies a negative shift up to layer 31 and positive shift from 32 onward for both metrics, which should satisfy the replication constraints; register_dissociation uses opposite shifts for LL and T to create a dissociation pattern; and entangled applies a positive shift to the final region of LL.

For split_not_replicated, the T metric stays positive everywhere, preventing replication from succeeding. The void world handles cases where the identity metric breaks or margins collapse. I'm also setting up kernel validation to check that the gate fires correctly when planted in the depth_commitment world with its decreasing profile.

Now I'm establishing the noise baseline around 0.06-0.08 with n=24 samples, which should give clear significance. For the construction primitives, I need to verify that all slot sequences match as distinct multisets, confirm the slot3 operation matches the expected transformation, validate the arm reuse against the builders, and ensure the band excludes the final layer. Then I'll write out the model path by extracting the band layer indices.

For the axes, I'm capturing residuals across all layers and using class axes to get the real member structure. For the per-layer T values, I'm projecting the hidden weights against these axes and computing the signed T statistic...

Writing T computation function... Writing the analysis loop... The residual tensors stay in float32 on CPU, while logit_lens converts to the model's native bfloat16 dtype for the matmul to match the model's own computation. Memory usage looks reasonable—logits at 65MB in float32 and 33MB in bfloat16 before conversion. For the identity check using the direct surprisal path, I'm computing the signed log-likelihood directly from the model's logits using the mean attribution scores.

For the real anchor, I'll use the direct path on "The {m}" for each real member, capturing axes with one forward pass per member and computing direct surprisals with eight forwards per member—72 forwards total, which is manageable. Across all nine arms with eight forwards each, that's roughly 80 forwards per nonce at 0.3-0.8 seconds each, so around 40-60 seconds per nonce. For 20 nonces, I'm looking at 15-20 minutes total. I'll add progress prints per nonce and save the results as an npz file.

For the tokenization, I'll get the input_ids list from the tokenizer without return_tensors, matching what tape_subtraction did. Qwen3 typically doesn't add a BOS token, which is consistent with the s328 instruments. The boundary handling between prefix and continuation text follows the same pattern as s328's surprisal computation—tokenizing them separately with space-prefixed continuations to avoid tokenization artifacts.
